=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums of length n and a 2D array queries where queries[i] = [l_i, r_i].
Each queries[i] represents the following action on nums:

Decrement the value at each index in the range [l_i, r_i] in nums by at most 1.
The amount by which the value is decremented can be chosen independently for each index.

A Zero Array is an array with all its elements equal to 0.
Return the maximum number of elements that can be removed from queries, such that nums can still be converted to a zero array using the remaining queries. If it is not possible to convert nums to a zero array, return -1.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,0,2], queries = [[0,2],[0,2],[1,1]]
Output: 1
Explanation:
After removing queries[2], nums can still be converted to a zero array.

Using queries[0], decrement nums[0] and nums[2] by 1 and nums[1] by 0.
Using queries[1], decrement nums[0] and nums[2] by 1 and nums[1] by 0.


Example 2:

Input: nums = [1,1,1,1], queries = [[1,3],[0,2],[1,3],[1,2]]
Output: 2
Explanation:
We can remove queries[2] and queries[3].

Example 3:

Input: nums = [1,2,3,4], queries = [[0,3]]
Output: -1
Explanation:
nums cannot be converted to a zero array even after using all the queries.

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 10^5
0 <= nums[i] <= 10^5
1 <= queries.length <= 10^5
queries[i].length == 2
0 <= l_i <= r_i < nums.length

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxRemoval(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Since if selected interval covers i, can choose to decrement or not. Need for each index independently choose nums[i] of the covering selected intervals to decrement. No coupling across indices except each query can decrement each index independently (at most 1 per index), so no global capacity. Thus yes, coverage count >= nums[i] is sufficient: for each index, pick any nums[i] selected intervals covering it. Therefore problem: Given intervals, find maximum number of intervals to remove such that remaining intervals have coverage c_i >= nums[i] for all i. Implementation details:
```
class Solution:
    def maxRemoval(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        import heapq
        n = len(nums)
        by_start = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for l, r in queries:
            by_start[l].append(r)
        available = []  # max-heap of right endpoints, store -r
        selected = []   # min-heap of right endpoints of selected active intervals
        kept = 0
        for i, need in enumerate(nums):
            for r in by_start[i]:
                heapq.heappush(available, -r)
            while selected and selected[0] < i:
                heapq.heappop(selected)
            while len(selected) < need:
                while available and -available[0] < i:
                    heapq.heappop(available)
                if not available:
                    return -1
                r = -heapq.heappop(available)
                heapq.heappush(selected, r)
                kept += 1
        return len(queries) - kept
```

But wait: Suppose selected heap contains intervals with r < i that are expired; we remove before checking. But what about selected intervals with r < i that are also in available? no. Good.

They will remain in available until a later i when need>0 or maybe never. If never, no issue. But if at later i, they are even more expired, inner while discards. If need=0 all along, available may accumulate expired intervals but memory q okay. However, if need=0 at current but later need>0, expired intervals from earlier remain; inner while discards. Fine. === ANSWER ===
should be final code only. But before final, I need ensure no issue with "commit-implement-recheck" not relevant. Final code block.

Let's ensure code uses `heapq` imported. We'll output:

```python
from typing import List
import heapq

class Solution:
    def maxRemoval(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        by_start = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for l, r in queries:
            by_start[l].append(r)

        available = []  # max-heap of right endpoints, stored as -r
        selected = []   # min-heap of right endpoints of kept active intervals
        kept = 0

        for i, need in enumerate(nums):
            for r in by_start[i]:
                heapq.heappush(available, -r)

            while selected and selected[0] < i:
                heapq.heappop(selected)

            while len(selected) < need:
                while available and -available[0] < i:
                    heapq.heappop(available)
                if not available:
                    return -1
                r = -heapq.heappop(available)
                heapq.heappush(selected, r)
                kept += 1

        return len(queries) - kept
```

Need consider if `by_start` list of lists if n maybe 0? constraints n>=1. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with `while len(selected) < need:` if need is huge and available contains intervals but some are expired not top? We pop top usable; after all usable popped, top expired, while discards. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with `available` heap containing intervals with r < i and need=0; they remain. If later need>0, while discards. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with `selected` heap containing intervals with r < i and need=0; we remove before while. Good.

Now, final answer code block only.</think>```python
from typing import List
import heapq

class Solution:
    def maxRemoval(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        by_start = [[] for _ in range(n)]

        for l, r in queries:
            by_start[l].append(r)

        available = []  # max-heap of right endpoints, stored as -r
        selected = []   # min-heap of right endpoints of kept active intervals
        kept = 0

        for i, need in enumerate(nums):
            for r in by_start[i]:
                heapq.heappush(available, -r)

            while selected and selected[0] < i:
                heapq.heappop(selected)

            while len(selected) < need:
                while available and -available[0] < i:
                    heapq.heappop(available)

                if not available:
                    return -1

                r = -heapq.heappop(available)
                heapq.heappush(selected, r)
                kept += 1

        return len(queries) - kept
```
